      readonly capabilities: RendererCapabilities;
      create(canvas: HTMLCanvasElement, options?: { readonly shaders?: ShaderPack }): Renderer;
    }
Used by: spacelab.flight-split.

## OverlayPrimitive
    export type OverlayPrimitive =
      /** `fill` paints the closed outline instead of stroking it; the shape must be convex. */
      | { readonly kind: 'line'; readonly space: OverlaySpace; readonly points: readonly Vec[]; readonly color: string;
          readonly width?: number; readonly closed?: boolean; readonly fill?: boolean }
      /** Radius is metres in world space and CSS pixels in screen space. */
      | { readonly kind: 'circle'; readonly space: OverlaySpace; readonly center: Vec; readonly radius: number;
          readonly color: string; readonly width?: number; readonly fill?: boolean }
      | { readonly kind: 'text'; readonly space: OverlaySpace; readonly at: Vec; readonly text: string; readonly color: string;
          readonly align?: 'left' | 'center' | 'right' };
Used by: builtin.maneuver-planner, builtin.navball, spacelab.render-canvas, spacelab.render-webgl, spacelab.throttle-hud, spacelab.view-hud.

## PresentationSnapshot
A detached, frozen snapshot of what the pilot sees. Built at most once per frame.
    export interface PresentationSnapshot {
      /** Interpolated presentation time (seconds) and the last committed simulation tick. */
      readonly time: number;
      readonly tick: number;
      readonly paused: boolean;
      /** Wall seconds since the previous frame. Presentation only; never feeds the simulation. */
      readonly elapsed: number;
      readonly viewport: { readonly width: number; readonly height: number; readonly pixelRatio: number };
      readonly body: PresentationBody;
      readonly ship?: PresentationShip;
      readonly vehicle: Readonly<VehicleDefaults>;
      /** The active motion model provides analytic orbital diagnostics. */
      readonly analyticOrbits: boolean;
      /** Analytic elements of a flying ship's orbit, when `analyticOrbits`. */
      readonly orbit: Elements | null;
      /** Height above local ground or sea in metres (negative underwater). */
      readonly groundClearance: number | null;
      readonly prediction: { readonly status: GhostEnd | 'tracing' | 'none'; readonly samples: number; readonly last?: GhostSample };
      /** Optional local flight plan. Body-relative SI coordinates; a preview never changes live motion. */
      readonly maneuver?: ManeuverPreview;
      /** Shared-world rule namespaces at the last committed tick. */
      readonly moduleState: Readonly<Record<string, WorldRuleState>>;
    }
Used by: builtin.maneuver-planner, builtin.navball.

## ManeuverPreview
    export interface ManeuverPreview {
      /** Completed nodes remain in the editor journal but no longer draw burn targets. */
      readonly completed?: boolean;
      readonly nodes?: readonly { readonly id: string; readonly time: number; readonly position: Vec; readonly label: string; readonly selected: boolean; readonly completed?: boolean; readonly valid: boolean; readonly deltaVelocity?: Vec; readonly throttleBurnSeconds?: number | null }[];
      readonly time: number;
      readonly position: Vec;
      readonly deltaVelocity: Vec;
      readonly burnSeconds: number;
      /** Estimate at the current throttle setting, even with engines off. Null means no thrust. */
      readonly throttleBurnSeconds?: number | null;
      readonly throttle?: number;
      readonly remainingDeltaV?: number;
      readonly valid: boolean;
      readonly path: readonly GhostSample[];
    }
Used by: builtin.maneuver-planner, spacelab.maneuver-editor.

## interpolateState
Presentation snapshot `alpha` of the way from one fixed physics step to the next, so motion stays smooth when 10-ms steps do not divide the display's frame interval. Visual only: physics must never read the result.
    export function interpolateState(from: State, to: State, alpha: number): State;
Used by: spacelab.fleet-view, spacelab.spin-view.

## hermiteControls
Bézier control points of the cubic Hermite curve between two timed states. Exact end positions and velocities keep sparse quantized samples smooth at any zoom; the projection is affine, so world-space control points project directly.
    export function hermiteControls(a: GhostSample, b: GhostSample): readonly [Vec, Vec];

## pathFromShip
The drawn part of a path. Open paths drop samples the ship has passed and begin at the ship; `flown` is the chord length already behind it, which keeps the dashes world-fixed.
    export function pathFromShip(path: readonly GhostSample[], closed: boolean,
      ship: GhostSample): { readonly points: readonly GhostSample[]; readonly flown: number };
Used by: spacelab.render-canvas, spacelab.render-webgl.

## trajectoryMarkers
Restrained timed SOI markers and future body outlines. All snapshots are in the path's translating reference frame, so apply pathOrigin exactly once. Old providers can omit encounterBody. No renderer-side physics/ephemeris queries.
    export function trajectoryMarkers(frame: RenderFrame): OverlayPrimitive[];

=== game-api: services.ts [api-reference/game-api/services]

## ProviderIdentity
    export interface ProviderIdentity {
      readonly id: string;
      readonly version: string;
      /** All parameters affecting behavior; local identity, not a package fingerprint. */
      readonly modelIdentity: string;
    }
Used by: builtin.motion.

## OrbitalProvider
Analytic providers promise two-body propagation and conservative radius events. A provider without that verified contract sets analytic=false; ordinary motion remains usable.
    export interface OrbitalProvider extends ProviderIdentity {
      readonly analytic: boolean;
      elements(state: OrbitalState, mu: number): Elements;
      propagate(state: OrbitalState, mu: number, seconds: number, options?: SolverOptions): Propagation;
      timeToRadius(state: OrbitalState, mu: number, radius: number, direction: 'inbound' | 'outbound', elements?: Elements): number | null;
    }
Used by: builtin.motion.

## SurfaceProvider
All geometry uses the public radial SurfaceProfile contract. Query, contact and generation must agree, including preset placement, water and prediction.
    export interface SurfaceProvider extends ProviderIdentity {
      generate(body: Body): SurfaceProfile;
      identity(body: Body): string;
      validate(body: Body, profile: SurfaceProfile): void;
      query(profile: SurfaceProfile, angle: number): SurfaceQuery;
      support(profile: SurfaceProfile, angle: number): SurfaceSupport;
      sweep(profile: SurfaceProfile, start: Vec, end: Vec, options?: SurfaceSweepOptions): SurfaceSweep;
      sweepGround(profile: SurfaceProfile, start: Vec, end: Vec, options?: SurfaceSweepOptions): SurfaceSweep;
    }
Used by: builtin.motion.

## BodyFrameProvider
Owns ephemerides, coordinate changes, SOI selection and preset placement. Returned systems are isolated per world; mutable derived ephemeris caches are permitted.
    export interface BodyFrameProvider extends ProviderIdentity {
      load(universe: UniverseDefinition, systemId: string, surfaces: SurfaceProvider): LoadedSystem;
    }
Used by: builtin.motion.

## RailsProvider
Sleep requires an explicit compatible binding and a conservative event horizon. 0 means unknown; Infinity is allowed only when no event is possible. The world additionally checks idle controls, contact and exact fixed-point eligibility. Evaluation uses the selected orbital provider and the context's guarded coastAt.
    export interface RailsProvider extends ProviderIdentity {
      readonly compatible: readonly RailsBinding[];
      horizon(system: LoadedSystem, body: ResolvedBody, state: OrbitalState, epoch: OrbitalState, time: number, top: number): number;
    }
Used by: builtin.motion.

## ChatLine

Used by: spacelab.session-flight.

## SessionRead
What the flight loop needs from a game, whether this browser owns the world or follows someone else's.
    export interface SessionRead {
      readonly kind: 'local' | 'client';
      readonly world: WorldRead;
      readonly playerId: string;
      readonly paused: boolean;
      readonly pauseReason: string;
      readonly warp: number;
      readonly requestedWarp: number;
      readonly warpReason: string;
      /** True if this seat controls time warp (only the host can activate warp). */
      readonly canWarp: boolean;
      setWarp(factor: number): void;
      /** Presentation fraction of the next fixed step. */
      readonly alpha: number;
      /** Ship record before the latest fixed step, for interpolation. */
      previous(shipId: string): Ship | undefined;
      /** Fading body-relative display correction; zero for local worlds. */
      displayOffset(shipId: string): Vec;
      /** Advance by wall-clock seconds. Held controls apply to `shipId` only.
       * On a local normal-speed tick, beforeTick runs first (owned fleet commands),
       * then beforeStep may replace the selected ship controls. Neither runs on rails. */
      update(seconds: number, shipId: string | undefined, controls: Controls, beforeStep?: () => Controls, beforeTick?: () => void): WorldEvent[];
      /** Returns the new ship's id when it exists immediately (local worlds). */
      spawn(presetId: string, godMode: boolean, vehicleId?: string): string | undefined;
      respawn(shipId: string, presetId: string): void;
      /** Place an owned ship (throw, orbit and teleport cheats). Local worlds apply it now or
       * throw a SessionError; remote hosts check it and answer with the next snapshot or a notice. */
      teleport(shipId: string, bodyId: string, position: Vec, velocity: Vec, heading?: number): void;
      remove(shipId: string): void;
      setThrottle(shipId: string, throttle: number): void;
      setGodMode(shipId: string, enabled: boolean): void;
      setGodModeAll(enabled: boolean): void;
      /** A module command for an owned ship. Local worlds apply it now through the host's
       * checks (or throw a SessionError); remote results arrive with the next snapshot. */
      command(shipId: string, commandId: string, args?: Readonly<Record<string, number>>): void;
      players(): readonly SessionPlayer[];
      playerName(id: string): string | undefined;
      /** Say something to everyone in this world (just this pilot when playing alone). */
      chat(text: string): void;
      dataCommand(request: ModuleDataCommand): void;
      /** Every chat line in this world, this pilot's own included. */
      onchat: ((line: ChatLine) => void) | null;
    }
Used by: spacelab.flight-split, spacelab.session-flight.

      readonly now?: () => number;
      readonly lock?: AuthoritativeLock | ModuleLock; readonly simulationFingerprint?: string;
      readonly rules?: readonly WorldRuleExtension[]; readonly forces?: readonly ForceExtension[]; readonly commands?: readonly CommandExtension[];

      readonly verifyCandidate?: (candidate: {
        lock?: AuthoritativeLock | ModuleLock; fingerprint?: string; universe: UniverseDefinition; systemId: string;
      }) => { ok: boolean; reason?: string; simulationFingerprint?: string } | boolean;

## FoundSession
Discovered server endpoint record with verified protocol compatibility.
    export interface FoundSession {
      /** Target server URL or connection address. */

      /** Human-readable session or world name. */

      /** Current count of connected players. */
      readonly players: number;
      /** Maximum players accepted by the host. */

      readonly password: boolean;

      readonly compatible: boolean;
    }

## LanScanOptions
Options controlling a concurrent discovery scan across candidate addresses.
    export interface LanScanOptions {
      /** Maximum number of simultaneous probes (1–64, default 32). */
      readonly concurrency?: number;
      /** Timeout per candidate probe in milliseconds (1–60000, default 800). */
      readonly timeoutMs?: number;
      /** Abort signal cancelling pending and future candidate probes. */
      readonly signal?: AbortSignal;
      /** Callback invoked as each compatible or incompatible server responds. */
      readonly onfound?: (server: FoundSession) => void;
      /** Progress callback reporting (probesCompleted, totalCandidates). */
      readonly onprogress?: (done: number, total: number) => void;
    }
Used by: spacelab.discovery-lan.

    export interface LanDiscoveryOptions {
      /** Default probe timeout in milliseconds. */

      readonly concurrency?: number;
      /** Maximum characters accepted in a discovery greeting before aborting (default 16384). */
      readonly maxGreetingLength?: number;
      /** Borrowed transport provider used to establish probe connections. */
      readonly transport: TransportProvider;
      /** Expected session protocol identifier (e.g. `space-sim-flight/1`). */

      readonly modelId?: string;

## LanDiscovery

    export interface LanDiscovery extends Disposable {

=== game-api: shader-format.ts [api-reference/game-api/shader-format]
Declarations from shader-format.ts used by documented modules.

## validateShaderDefinition
Validate, bound and freeze a shader pack definition against baseline defaults. Partial packs inherit built-in stages, never stages from a previously loaded custom pack. @param value Raw object representation of a shader pack. @param defaults Baseline defaults providing fallback stages for omitted passes. @returns Frozen, validated ShaderPack with resolved passes. @throws If format differs from 'space-sim-shaders/1', stages exceed 256 KiB, or fields are invalid.
    export function validateShaderDefinition(value: unknown, defaults: ShaderPack): ShaderPack;
Used by: spacelab.render-webgl, spacelab.vector-shaders.

## parseShaderDefinition
Parse and validate a GLSL fragment file or JSON shader pack string. A .frag/.glsl file replaces just the scene fragment; JSON can replace all six stages. @param text Source file text (UTF-8, up to 1 MiB). @param filename Source filename used for extension detection and naming. @param defaults Baseline defaults providing fallback stages. @returns Frozen, validated ShaderPack with resolved passes. @throws If file exceeds 1 MiB or shader syntax validation fails.
    export function parseShaderDefinition(text: string, filename: string, defaults: ShaderPack): ShaderPack;
Used by: spacelab.render-webgl, spacelab.vector-shaders.
-> API reference index [api-reference]

## State
A flying state is in the body-centred inertial frame. On a rotating body a landed or crashed state is in the body-fixed frame instead: it rests with constant position and heading.
    export interface State {
      readonly moduleState?: Readonly<Record<string, ModuleState>>;
      readonly time: number;
      readonly position: Vec;
      readonly velocity: Vec;
      readonly heading: number;
      readonly throttle: number;
      readonly status: 'landed' | 'flying' | 'crashed';
      readonly revision: number;
      readonly godMode?: boolean;
      readonly coast?: Coast;
      readonly dynamics?: 'kepler' | 'numerical';
      readonly solver?: SolverDiagnostics;
      /** Terrain event epoch lies within this completed scheduler step. */
      readonly contact?: SurfaceContactEvent;
      readonly water?: 'submerged' | 'floating';
      /** Split drift vertex retained for prediction of a gentle sea entry. */
      readonly waterEntry?: GhostSample;
    }
Used by: spacelab.fleet-view, spacelab.flight-plans, spacelab.flight-prediction, spacelab.flight-split, spacelab.maneuver-burn, spacelab.maneuver-editor, spacelab.render-canvas, spacelab.render-webgl, spacelab.spin-view, spacelab.transfer-planner.

## GhostSample
    export interface GhostSample extends Sample {
      readonly velocity: Vec;
      /** Segment ending here is a verified numerical drift, not a conic/Hermite arc. */
      readonly linear?: boolean;
      readonly contactMaterial?: 'water';
      /** Active gravitational body. Multi-body forecasts express position/velocity in
       * frameId's translating, nonrotating frame at this sample's time. */
      readonly bodyId?: string;
      readonly frameId?: string;
      /** First sample in a new SOI; continuous position, velocity and time. */
      readonly encounter?: { readonly from: string; readonly to: string };
      /** Destination body at this SOI handoff time, in the same translating frame as
       * position. Optional display snapshot, never current world coordinates. The
       * reference radius is not a terrain/contact boundary. */
      readonly encounterBody?: { readonly position: Vec; readonly radius: number; readonly name: string };
    }
Used by: builtin.maneuver-planner, spacelab.flight-prediction, spacelab.maneuver-editor, spacelab.transfer-planner.

## SurfaceProfile
Private Float64 storage; reads/copies cannot mutate authoritative geometry. No cache or browser globals. Callers own profile lifetimes.
    export interface SurfaceProfile {
      readonly identity: string;
      readonly referenceRadius: number;
      readonly orientationRadians: number;
      readonly sampleCount: number;
      readonly seaRadiusMetres?: number;
      /** Exact polar-linear sea crossings, wrapped to [0, 2*pi). */
      readonly coastAngles: readonly number[];
      readonly hasWater: boolean;
      readonly bounds: SurfaceBounds;
      readonly features: readonly SurfaceFeature[];
      readonly pads: readonly SurfacePadDefinition[];
      /** Elevations plus three interval arrays; excludes JS objects and identity text. */
      readonly storageBytes: number;
      sampleElevation(index: number): number;
      intervalBounds(index: number): SurfaceBounds;
      copyElevations(): Float64Array;
    }
Used by: spacelab.flight-prediction, spacelab.render-canvas, spacelab.render-webgl.
-> API reference index [api-reference]

=== game-api: tab-transport.ts [api-reference/game-api/tab-transport]
Declarations from tab-transport.ts used by documented modules.

Import these names from @space-sim/game-api. Units are SI unless a note says otherwise. Source: packages/game-api/src/tab-transport.ts.

## TabChannel
Message channel abstraction for intra-browser communication (e.g. `BroadcastChannel`).
    export interface TabChannel {
      /** Post a message object across all listeners on this channel name. */
      postMessage(message: unknown): void;
      /** Close and release this channel instance. */
      close(): void;
      /** Handler receiving incoming message events. */
      onmessage: ((event: { readonly data: unknown }) => void) | null;
    }
Used by: spacelab.net-tab.

## TabLocks
Web Locks API abstraction for leader election and mutual exclusion between browser tabs.
    export interface TabLocks {
      /** Request an exclusive or shared lock by name. */
      request(name: string, options: { readonly signal?: AbortSignal }, callback: () => Promise<void>): Promise<unknown>;
    }
Used by: spacelab.net-tab.

## TabNetworkOptions
Options for configuring a scoped same-origin tab network instance.
    export interface TabNetworkOptions {
      /** Namespace or game scope partitioning BroadcastChannels and Web Locks. */
      readonly scope: string;
      /** Session protocol identifier (e.g. `space-sim-flight/1`). */
      readonly protocolId: string;
      /** Optional channel factory. Defaults to native `BroadcastChannel` in browser environments. */
      readonly channel?: (name: string) => TabChannel;
      /** Optional lock adapter. Defaults to `navigator.locks`. Null disables Web Locks. */
      readonly locks?: TabLocks | null;
      /** Heartbeat interval in milliseconds when Web Locks are unavailable. */
      readonly beatMs?: number;
      /** Inactivity timeout in milliseconds before considering an idle peer disconnected. */
      readonly timeoutMs?: number;
      /** Maximum wait in milliseconds for a host tab to answer a connection handshake. */
      readonly connectTimeoutMs?: number;
      /** Maximum message text length in UTF-16 code units. */
      readonly maxTextLength?: number;
    }
Used by: spacelab.net-tab.

## TabAdvert
Broadcast advertisement published by a hosting tab.
    export interface TabAdvert {
      /** 32-character hexadecimal host identifier. */
      readonly host: string;
      /** Human-readable session name. */
      readonly name: string;
      /** Unique session identifier. */
      readonly sessionId: string;
      /** Current count of players in the session. */
      readonly players: number;
      /** Maximum players accepted by the host. */
      readonly maxPlayers: number;
    }
Used by: spacelab.net-tab.

## TabDiscovery
Active discovery subscription observing tab session advertisements.
    export interface TabDiscovery extends Disposable {
      /** Return snapshot of currently active tab session adverts. */
      adverts(): readonly TabAdvert[];
    }
Used by: spacelab.net-tab.

## TabNetwork
Tab transport network instance providing scoped peer-to-peer connections and discovery across browser tabs.
    export interface TabNetwork extends TransportProvider, Disposable {
      /** Scope partitioning this tab network. */
      readonly scope: string;
      /** Session protocol identifier. */
      readonly protocolId: string;
      /** Discovery provider for scanning advertised sessions within this scope. */
      readonly discovery: DiscoveryProvider;
      /** Advertise and host a session from this tab, accepting incoming peer connections. */
      host(describe: () => Omit<TabAdvert, 'host'>, accept: (peer: TextConnection) => void, signal: AbortSignal): TabHost;
      /** Watch for active tab hosts, invoking change callback whenever adverts update. */
      watch(change: (adverts: readonly TabAdvert[]) => void, signal: AbortSignal): TabDiscovery;
    }
Used by: spacelab.net-tab.

## TabNetworkFactory
Factory creating scoped TabNetwork instances.
    export interface TabNetworkFactory {
      /** Create a scoped tab network instance with the provided options. */
      create(options: TabNetworkOptions): TabNetwork;
    }
Used by: spacelab.net-tab.

## TAB_TRANSPORT
ServiceKey for the scoped tab transport factory (slot `net.tab`).
    export const TAB_TRANSPORT: ServiceKey<TabNetworkFactory> = Object.freeze({ slot: 'net.tab', version: '1.0.0' });
Used by: spacelab.net-tab.

=== game-api: theme.ts [api-reference/game-api/theme]
Declarations from theme.ts used by documented modules.

Import these names from @space-sim/game-api. Units are SI unless a note says otherwise. Source: packages/game-api/src/theme.ts.

## ThemeSettings
Presentation theme settings specifying backdrop, celestial grid, HUD and window styling.
    export interface ThemeSettings extends RenderThemeOptions {
      /** Visual style for the procedural backdrop (e.g. 'spacelab', 'nebula', 'carina', 'void'). */
      backdropStyle: ThemeBackdropStyle;
      /** Whether procedural nebulae are rendered in the background. */
      nebulaVisible: boolean;
      /** Relative brightness and opacity of procedural nebulae (0.0 to 2.0). */
      nebulaIntensity: number;
      /** Whether background star layers are rendered. */
      starsVisible: boolean;
      /** Multiplier for procedural background star count (0.5 to 2.0). */
      starDensity: number;
      /** Whether interstellar dust clouds are visible. */
      dustVisible: boolean;
      /** Whether distant deep-space galaxies are visible. */
      galaxiesVisible: boolean;
      /** Whether procedural nebulae animate slowly over presentation time. */
      animatedNebula: boolean;
      /** Randomization seed for background generation; 0 uses the universe seed. */
      shaderSeed: number;

      /** Whether the coordinate / navigation grid is displayed. */
      gridEnabled: boolean;
      /** Grid projection style ('cartesian', 'polar', 'dots', 'hex', 'off'). */
      gridStyle: ThemeGridStyle;
      /** Grid spacing in CSS pixels (e.g. 80). */
      gridSpacing: number;
      /** Visual opacity of grid lines and markers (0.1 to 1.0). */
      gridOpacity: number;
      /** Whether coordinate values and distance labels are displayed on grid lines. */
      gridLabels: boolean;
      /** Optional custom hex color string for the grid (e.g. '#38bdf8'). */
      gridColor?: string;

      /** Visual style for digital counters and telemetry ('spacelab', 'cyber', 'apollo', etc.). */
      counterTheme: ThemeCounterStyle;
      /** Custom phosphor / telemetry accent color (e.g. '#00f0ff'). */
      hudColor?: string;
      /** Window framing style ('classic', 'cyber', 'apollo', 'tactical', etc.). */
      windowStyle?: ThemeWindowStyle;
      /** Opacity for translucent UI windows and panels (0.4 to 1.0). */
      windowOpacity: number;
      /** Custom window header and border accent color. */
      windowColor?: string;
      /** Whether retro CRT scanlines overlay the flight display. */
      hudScanlines: boolean;
      /** Whether phosphor HUD elements display a diffuse glow. */
      hudGlow: boolean;
      /** Whether HUD instruments and telemetry panels have chamfered corners. */
      hudChamfer: boolean;
      /** Whether HUD widgets collapse into a compact layout for small viewports. */
      compactHud: boolean;
      /** Whether full-resolution procedural textures and anti-aliasing are enabled. */
      highQuality: boolean;
    }
Used by: spacelab.theme, spacelab.theme-customizer.

    export interface ThemePreset {

## ThemePreferences

    export interface ThemePreferences {
      /** Retrieve a stored JSON preference string by key, or null if unset. */
      getItem(key: string): string | null;
      /** Store a preference string under the specified key. */
      setItem(key: string, value: string): void;
    }
Used by: spacelab.theme, spacelab.theme-customizer.

## ThemeOptions

    export interface ThemeOptions {
      /** Optional persistent storage backend; defaults to in-memory preferences when omitted. */
      readonly storage?: ThemePreferences;
      /** Optional DOM element whose CSS custom properties are updated on theme change. */
      readonly root?: HTMLElement;
      /** Initial fallback defaults overriding built-in settings. */
      readonly defaults?: Partial<ThemeSettings>;
    }
Used by: spacelab.theme.

## ThemeController
Controller managing active theme settings, CSS variables, procedural backdrop drawing and observer subscriptions.
    export interface ThemeController extends ThemeService, ThemeArtwork, Disposable {
      /** Immutable default settings configured on controller instantiation. */
      readonly defaults: Readonly<ThemeSettings>;
      /** Built-in curated presets available for user selection. */
      readonly presets: readonly ThemePreset[];
      /** Retrieve the currently active theme settings. */
      current(): Readonly<ThemeSettings>;
      /** Reload theme settings from persistent storage and apply styling to the DOM root. */
      load(): Readonly<ThemeSettings>;
      /** Apply current styling to newly mounted children without changing settings. */
      refresh(): void;
    }
Used by: spacelab.theme, spacelab.theme-customizer.

## ThemeFactory

    export interface ThemeFactory extends Disposable {

      create(options?: ThemeOptions): ThemeController;
    }

=== game-api: transfers.ts [api-reference/game-api/transfers]
Declarations from transfers.ts used by documented modules.

Import these names from @space-sim/game-api. Units are SI unless a note says otherwise. Source: packages/game-api/src/transfers.ts.

## TransferRequest
Transfer window search request specifying ship, destination body, target altitudes in SI metres, and duration limits.
    export interface TransferRequest {
     readonly shipId: string; readonly destinationId: string;
     /** SI metres above the destination's reference radius. Equal values select a circle. */
     readonly periapsisAltitude: number; readonly apoapsisAltitude: number;
     /** Optional total search horizon from the source epoch, in SI seconds. */
     readonly maxFlightSeconds?: number;
     /** Staged routes park in a high orbit beside the neighbouring moon when true or
      * omitted; false keeps low parking near each stop body. Honoured when the provider
      * declares parkingOrbits. */
     readonly highParking?: boolean;
    }
Used by: spacelab.transfer-planner.

## TransferOption
Evaluated transfer route candidate providing flight plan nodes, burn times, delta-v breakdown in m/s, and route structure.
    export interface TransferOption {
     readonly kind: 'efficient' | 'faster'; readonly plan: FlightPlan;
     readonly waitSeconds: number; readonly travelSeconds: number;
     readonly deltaV: number; readonly departureDeltaV: number; readonly arrivalDeltaV: number;
     readonly departureBurnSeconds: number; readonly arrivalBurnSeconds: number;
     readonly warning: string;
     /** Optional whole-route summary. IDs resolve through the same world hierarchy as the picker. */
     readonly route?: { readonly bodyIds:readonly string[];readonly stops:readonly string[];readonly maxFlightSeconds:number;readonly finiteValidated:boolean;readonly burns?:readonly {readonly nodeId:string;readonly deltaV:number;readonly seconds:number}[] };
    }
Used by: spacelab.maneuver-editor, spacelab.transfer-planner.

## TransferProgress
Intermediate transfer search progress describing current phase, candidate counts, completion fraction, and best options so far.
    export interface TransferProgress {
     /** Optional explanation of the leg or whole-route validation currently running. */
     readonly phase?: string;
     readonly tested: number; readonly total: number; readonly accepted: number;
     /** Optional whole-search completion estimate in [0, 1], nondecreasing for one search.
      * tested/total may describe only the current leg or phase; hosts show progress as
      * indeterminate when this is absent. */
     readonly fraction?: number;
     /** Fully validated best options so far; optional for existing providers.
      * A new array signals changed results. Search may continue after publication. */
     readonly options?: readonly TransferOption[];
    }
Used by: spacelab.maneuver-editor, spacelab.transfer-planner.

## TransferResult
Final result of a bounded transfer search returning discovered options and an explanation if empty.
    export interface TransferResult {
     /** Fully validated whole-route alternatives, including those dominated by the displayed options. */
     readonly compared?: {readonly direct:number;readonly staged:number};
     readonly options: readonly TransferOption[]; readonly reason: string }
Used by: spacelab.maneuver-editor, spacelab.transfer-planner.

## TransferRelationship
Inert bounded search; each next() performs one bounded candidate operation or shared forecast yield. return() cancels owned forecasts/contexts. No controls or live stepping.
    export type TransferRelationship = 'same-body' | 'child' | 'parent' | 'sibling' | 'other';
Used by: spacelab.maneuver-editor, spacelab.transfer-planner.

## TransferPlanner
Generator-based bounded orbital transfer solver evaluating same-body and interbody trajectories (`flight.transfers@1.0.0`).
    export interface TransferPlanner {
     /** Bind the host-selected controller without a dependency on a higher module tier. */
     withBurns?(burns:ManeuverBurnFactory):TransferPlanner;
     /** Optional solver coverage for destination browsing. Absent: the search explains
      * suitability. A relationship being listed is not a feasibility guarantee. */
     readonly relationships?: readonly TransferRelationship[];
     /** Optional bounds for an explicit mission-duration input. */
     readonly maxFlightSeconds?:number;
     readonly defaultFlightSeconds?:number;
     /** True when search honours TransferRequest.highParking for intermediate stops. */
     readonly parkingOrbits?:boolean;
     search(world: WorldRead, request: TransferRequest): Generator<TransferProgress, TransferResult>;
    }
Used by: spacelab.maneuver-editor, spacelab.transfer-planner.

## TRANSFERS
Service key for the orbital transfer planner (`flight.transfers@1.0.0`).
    export const TRANSFERS: ServiceKey<TransferPlanner> = Object.freeze({ slot: 'flight.transfers', version: '1.0.0' });
Used by: spacelab.maneuver-editor, spacelab.transfer-planner.

=== game-api: view-hud.ts [api-reference/game-api/view-hud]
Declarations from view-hud.ts used by documented modules.

Import these names from @space-sim/game-api. Units are SI unless a note says otherwise. Source: packages/game-api/src/view-hud.ts.

## HudBinding
Per-view binding tying a HUD view to a seat, viewport, session, and optional context.
    export interface HudBinding extends LocalViewBinding { readonly session: object; readonly context?: object }
Used by: spacelab.player-view, spacelab.view-hud.

## HudSurface
The pane an instrument draws in. Sizes and offsets are CSS pixels; `root` is the DOM parent the instrument may append its own elements to and must remove them on dispose.
    export interface HudSurface { readonly root: HTMLElement; readonly width: number; readonly height: number; readonly x: number; readonly y: number }
Used by: spacelab.throttle-hud, spacelab.view-hud.

## HudInstrumentContext
Per-instrument initialization context providing access to declared required adapters.
    export interface HudInstrumentContext {
      readonly binding: HudBinding;
      /** Returns an adapter listed in the definition's `requires`; throws for any other key. */
      use<T>(key: ServiceKey<T>): T;
    }
Used by: builtin.navball.

## HudInstrument
One instrument instance for one seat and viewport, owned by a ViewHud. A method that throws disables only this instrument: the owner cancels and disposes it and reports the fault.
    export interface HudInstrument extends Disposable {
      /** Stop any pointer or keyboard gesture in progress; a new gesture is needed afterwards. */
      cancel(): void;
      /** The seat's session, player or target changed. Gestures were cancelled first. */
      rebind(binding: HudBinding): void;
      resize(surface: HudSurface): void;
      /** Once per rendered frame. `elapsed` is wall-clock seconds since the previous frame,
       * not simulation time; do not advance or write the world here. */
      update(elapsed: number): void;
      /** Append overlay shapes for this frame; shapes appended before a throw are discarded. */
      draw(into: OverlayPrimitive[]): void;
    }
Used by: builtin.navball, spacelab.view-hud.

## HudInstrumentDefinition
A selectable instrument, published as a service such as `THROTTLE_HUD`. Stateless and reusable: the HUD owner calls `create` once per seat and disposes each instance.
    export interface HudInstrumentDefinition {
      /** Namespaced ID, such as `spacelab.throttle-hud`; unique within one selection. */
      readonly id: string;
      /** Exact-version, read-only view adapters. An incompatible view omits this contribution. */
      readonly requires: readonly Pick<ServiceKey<unknown>, 'slot' | 'version'>[];
      create(context: HudInstrumentContext): HudInstrument;
    }
Used by: builtin.navball, spacelab.throttle-hud, spacelab.view-hud.

## ViewHud

      readonly binding: HudBinding;
      readonly count: number;
      bind(binding: HudBinding): void;
      select(instruments: readonly HudInstrumentDefinition[]): void;
      /** Cancel instrument gestures without removing the current selection. */
      cancel?(): void;

## VIEW_HUD
Service key for the shared per-view HUD factory (`ui.view-hud@1.0.0`).
    export const VIEW_HUD: ServiceKey<ViewHudFactory> = Object.freeze({slot:'ui.view-hud',version:'1.0.0'});
Used by: spacelab.view-hud.

## ThrottleLeverState
Throttle readout for one seat. `throttle` is a fraction from 0 to 1; Space Sim reports 0 while the engine is not firing. `locked` means the host is ignoring throttle input, for example while paused. `world` and `shipId` identify the target so a gesture can detect a switch.
    export interface ThrottleLeverState {
      readonly world: object; readonly shipId: string; readonly throttle: number; readonly locked: boolean; readonly firing: boolean;
    }
Used by: spacelab.throttle-hud.

## ThrottleLeverOptions
Keyboard increments as fractions of full thrust in (0, 1]. Defaults: 0.01 and 0.1.
    export interface ThrottleLeverOptions { readonly keyboardStep?: number; readonly pageStep?: number }
Used by: spacelab.throttle-hud.

## FlightHudAdapter
Per-seat flight bridge supplied by the host as the `view.flight-hud-state` adapter. Reads return detached values or undefined when the seat has no flyable target.
    export interface FlightHudAdapter {
      /** Zero-based local seat index; Player 1 is 0. */
      readonly pilot: number;
      navball(): FlightHudReadout | undefined;
      throttle(): ThrottleLeverState | undefined;
      /** Fit the independent lever's preferred pane-local position around host HUD obstacles.
       * Both input and result are CSS pixels; callers must not retain the scratch input. */
      placeThrottle?(preferred: Vec, surface: HudSurface): Vec;
      /** Request thrust as a fraction from 0 to 1. The adapter rechecks session ownership, target
       * and input suppression at command time and ignores out-of-range or locked requests. */
      setThrottle(value: number): void;
      cancelGestures?(): void;
    }
Used by: builtin.navball.

## FLIGHT_HUD_STATE
Host adapter for flight instruments. Declare it in `HudInstrumentDefinition.requires`.
    export const FLIGHT_HUD_STATE: ServiceKey<FlightHudAdapter> = Object.freeze({slot:'view.flight-hud-state',version:'1.0.0'});
Used by: builtin.navball, spacelab.throttle-hud.

=== game-api: webrtc-transport.ts [api-reference/game-api/webrtc-transport]
Declarations from webrtc-transport.ts used by documented modules.

Import these names from @space-sim/game-api. Units are SI unless a note says otherwise. Source: packages/game-api/src/webrtc-transport.ts.

## ConnectionDiagnostics
Diagnostic metrics for a WebRTC peer connection. Exposes counts and state codes only; never IP addresses, raw SDP or secrets.
    export interface ConnectionDiagnostics {
      /** Current ICE gathering state (e.g. 'new', 'gathering', 'complete'). */
      readonly gathering: RTCIceGatheringState;
      /** Current peer connection state (e.g. 'new', 'connecting', 'connected', 'disconnected', 'failed', 'closed'). */
      readonly connection: RTCPeerConnectionState;
      /** Count of gathered ICE candidates grouped by candidate type (host, srflx, relay). */
      readonly candidates: Readonly<Record<string, number>>;
      /** Numerical error codes encountered during ICE or handshake. */
      readonly errors: readonly number[];
    }
Used by: spacelab.net-webrtc.

## ManualInvite
Active manual WebRTC invitation created by a session host.
    export interface ManualInvite extends Disposable {
      /** Compact base64url encoded invitation code containing compressed SDP offer. */
      readonly code: string;
      /** Whether STUN reflex candidate gathering was enabled for this invitation. */
      readonly stun: boolean;
      /** Return current connection and ICE gathering diagnostics. */
      diagnostics(): ConnectionDiagnostics;
      /** Accept a guest's reply code to finalize the connection. Resolves to the established TextConnection. */
      accept(reply: string): Promise<TextConnection>;
    }
Used by: spacelab.net-webrtc.

## ManualSignalingProvider
Manual WebRTC signaling provider exchanging copy-paste invite/reply codes for peer-to-peer data channels.
    export interface ManualSignalingProvider {
      /** Create a new invitation offer. Signal owns the invite lifecycle and pending gathering. */
      create(stun: boolean, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<ManualInvite>;
      /** Answer an invitation offer code, generating a reply code and pending connection promise. */
      answer(offer: string, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<ManualReply>;
      /** Validate whether text matches the expected invitation code format. */
      isInviteCode(text: string): boolean;
      /** Validate whether text matches the expected reply code format. */
      isReplyCode(text: string): boolean;
    }
Used by: spacelab.net-webrtc.

## Ship
One independently controlled vessel. `state` is relative to `bodyId`'s non-rotating frame.
    export interface Ship {
      /** Absent only on the legacy trainer path. Stable across respawn, replay and ownership. */
      readonly vehicleId?: string;
      readonly id: string;
      readonly name: string;
      readonly ownerId: string;
      readonly bodyId: string;
      readonly state: State;
      /** Held controls applied at every step until replaced. */
      readonly controls: Controls;
      /** Motion failed validation; the ship holds its last verified state until the fault is
       * cleared or it respawns. */
      readonly fault?: string;
    }

=== Alarms dock instrument [spacelab.alarm-panel/usage]
Optional read-only window over independent orbital clocks.


## What it does
This module contributes a dock instrument button and floating inspection window for viewing pending and delivered orbital alarms. It reads active simulation-time events from the flight alarm catalogue, formats relative countdowns and event descriptions, and presents polite live-region updates without scheduling, modifying, or executing flight commands.

## Provides and requires
- Provides view.alarms@1.0.0 through the ALARM_PANELS service key: creates AlarmPanel UI controller instances.
- Fills the view.alarms slot in platform profiles.
- Requires flight.alarm-catalogue@^1.0.0: queries active reminders and delivered status.
- Requires ui.windows@^1.0.0 (optional): creates floating chrome windows in supported window managers.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization resolves the alarm catalogue and optional window manager, registering ALARM_PANELS.
- create(options) mounts an Alarms window, adds the clock instrument to the dock shell, and binds change listeners.
- update() queries the catalogue at a throttled rate (minimum 200 ms between UI passes) to update countdowns and status text.
- dispose unregisters the dock instrument and closes the window chrome.

## Remove or replace
To customize alarm listing presentation or integrate with an alternative windowing framework, contribute an alternative provider for view.alarms@1.0.0 to the view.alarms slot. Removing this module removes the dock button and panel while underlying alarm clocks continue operating.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: DOM container structures and throttle interval timings may evolve prior to final API freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/alarm-panel demonstrates instantiating an alarm panel controller with a mock container and verifying rendered countdowns. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/alarm-panel/module.json /tmp/example-alarm-panel.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-alarm-panel.spacemod

=== Vehicle vector artwork [spacelab.trainer-appearance/usage]
Shared optional Canvas/WebGL hull and flame data.


## What it does
This module contributes the standard vector appearance for the orbital trainer ship (spacelab.trainer/orbit-trainer). It defines screen-space hull geometry (+X forward chevron outline with four vertices) and thruster flame geometry for Canvas 2D and WebGL 2 renderers. The artwork is purely cosmetic presentation data and never alters vessel mass, propulsion, collision bounds, flight mechanics or saved games.

## Provides and requires
- The module provides no service keys, requiring no runtime dependencies.
- Contributes a VehicleAppearance definition via context.contributeVehicleAppearance for vehicle ID spacelab.trainer/orbit-trainer.
- Contributes this usage guide for creators and the flight guide for players.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization contributes trainerAppearance to the module context and registers creator and player documentation guides.
- The module owns no persistent runtime state or active timers.
- Renderers retrieve vehicle appearance via createVehicleArtwork and draw the chevron hull and throttle flame during frame rendering.

## Remove or replace
Removing or replacing this module causes renderers to fall back to the generic four-point box marker (VEHICLE_MARKER) when drawing the trainer. Handling and flight characteristics remain unaffected. To provide alternative vehicle artwork, implement a presentation module calling contributeVehicleAppearance with custom geometry.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze. Vehicle ID is spacelab.trainer/orbit-trainer.

## Example
examples/modules/appearance-trainer demonstrates contributing custom vehicle vector appearance artwork and assembling a VehicleArtwork catalog. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/appearance-trainer/module.json /tmp/example-trainer-appearance.spacemod

=== Independent follow cameras [spacelab.camera-follow/usage]
Reusable basic camera control with an explicit flight adapter and optional smart-camera policy.


## What it does
This module provides independent follow and free camera controllers across local viewports. It tracks target positions and orientations in double-precision world coordinates, projects celestial bodies and vehicles to viewport CSS pixels, and manages smooth zoom, panning, inertial fling gestures, and horizon alignment without altering physical simulation stepping.

## Provides and requires
- Provides view.camera@1.0.0 through the VIEW_CAMERAS service key: creates LocalViewCamera instances for multi-view and presentation compositions.
- Provides view.flight-camera@1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_CAMERAS service key: provides the CameraController flight adapter supporting planet views, body-relative tracking, drag gestures, and rebasing.
- Requires view.smart-camera@1.0.0 (optional) through the SMART_CAMERAS service key: enables candidate search, guided arrival transitions, and focus acquisition when available.
- Fills the view.camera and view.flight-camera slots.

## Settings
- initialZoom (default 10, from 0.1 to 50): Starting magnification for each independent view. Changing a view later affects that view only.
- referenceMetres (default 1500, from 1 to 1000000000): World metres framed by the shorter viewport dimension at zoom 1. Changes presentation scale only.
- orientationSeconds (default 0.12, from 0 to 2, step 0.01): Wall seconds for camera rotation to approach the target orientation. Zero snaps immediately; simulation time is unchanged.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization captures initialZoom, referenceMetres, and orientationSeconds settings, queries optional SMART_CAMERAS, and contributes this guide.
- Selected slots register follow cameras for VIEW_CAMERAS and flight cameras for FLIGHT_CAMERAS.
- Each camera instance tracks zoom, pan velocity, orientation, and focus state, reprojecting frames during viewport reflow and rendering passes.
- Disposing a camera releases fling state and focus bindings; disposing the provider terminates all active camera instances.

## Remove or replace
To replace camera behavior, provide alternative services implementing view.camera@1.0.0 or view.flight-camera@1.0.0 in their respective slots. Removing this module without an alternative camera provider leaves viewports and flight splits without projection controllers.

## Stability
Module version 0.3.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: camera projection parameters, flight control hooks, and smart camera integration points may evolve before final API freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/camera-follow demonstrates instantiating a flight camera, adjusting magnification, and tracking target coordinates. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/camera-follow/module.json /tmp/example-camera-follow.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-camera-follow.spacemod

=== Smart camera add-on [spacelab.camera-smart/usage]
Optional target search, guided arrival and focus tracking for compatible cameras.


## What it does
This module provides optional targeting heuristics, guided arrival interpolations, and target tracking for compatible cameras. When a pilot flings or drags toward a cluster of vehicles or celestial bodies, the smart camera selects the most relevant candidate based on distance, screen velocity, and group priority, easing the view smoothly toward the acquired target.

## Provides and requires
- Provides view.smart-camera@1.0.0 through the SMART_CAMERAS service key: creates SmartCameraControl instances and candidate target selection functions.
- Fills the view.smart-camera slot in presentation configurations.
- Requires no external services.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization contributes this guide and registers the SMART_CAMERAS factory.
- create() produces independent SmartCameraControl instances per viewport.
- select(search) evaluates candidate target groups in prioritized order and returns the closest candidate matching trajectory vectors.
- start(target, followOnArrival) initiates guided camera movement toward the target's position until arrival, optionally handing off to follow mode.
- Disposing a control releases active tracking state; disposing the provider terminates all active smart camera controllers.

## Remove or replace
This module is completely optional. If removed or omitted from a profile, camera systems fall back to standard follow and drag behaviors without target snapping or guided arrival curves.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: candidate target scoring and guidance interfaces may be refined prior to API freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/camera-smart demonstrates selecting candidate targets and guiding a smart camera controller. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/camera-smart/module.json /tmp/example-camera-smart.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-camera-smart.spacemod

=== Chat console [spacelab.chat-console/usage]
Optional session chat and command UI with owned DOM lifecycle.


## What it does
This module renders the in-game text chat overlay and command entry console. It mounts an interactive UI into a caller-supplied container element, manages message history pruning up to a configured line count, supports keyboard shortcuts (Enter to focus, Slash to begin commands, Escape to minimize), displays message categories (system, error, success, player chat), and saves minimize state to local browser storage.

## Provides and requires
- Provides ui.chat@1.0.0 through the CHAT_CONSOLES key: a ChatConsoleFactory instance.
- Requires no services at module initialization.

## Settings
- historyLines (default 60, from 1 to 1000, step 1): Maximum messages retained in this local chat window. This does not change server chat history, permissions or message limits.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization provides the ChatConsoleFactory and registers it for owned disposal.
- create(options) mounts the chat HTML template and stylesheet into options.root, wiring keyboard listeners via AbortController.
- log(kind, text, sender) and show(line, playerId) format and append message rows, auto-scrolling to the bottom and pruning old lines.
- minimize(minimized, focusInput) toggles between full overlay and minimized pill views, persisting preference to space-sim:chat:minimized.
- clear() empties all current message lines from the log.
- Disposing the console aborts all DOM event listeners, removes injected stylesheets, and clears the container element.

## Remove or replace
Removing this module disables in-game chat and command console rendering. Dedicated servers and non-chat games operate normally without it. To replace it, author a presentation-tier module providing ui.chat@1.0.0 implementing ChatConsoleFactory.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.1 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze. Visual styling adopts standard Spacelab UI theme CSS custom properties.

## Example
examples/modules/chat-console demonstrates mounting a chat console, submitting text lines, and formatting message kinds. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/chat-console/module.json /tmp/chat-example.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/chat-example.spacemod

=== Controller menu [spacelab.controller-menu/usage]
Editable local-player and controller settings UI.


## What it does
This module renders the Players and Controllers tabs in the flight controls dialog. It provides the input.controller-menu service through the CONTROLLER_MENUS key. The Players tab configures local splitscreen multiplayer (1 to 4 seats), player names, input device assignments, and per-seat HUD navball locations. The Controllers tab configures stick deadzones, axis assignments and inversions, button mappings, and live hardware diagnostic meters. It operates gracefully without gamepad hardware, disabling live button capture while keeping layout editing fully functional. It does not advance simulation time or step physics worlds.

## Provides and requires
- Provides input.controller-menu@1.0.0 through the CONTROLLER_MENUS key: factory creating controller and local-player settings menus.
- Requires input.local-pilots@^1.0.0 for player seat preference codecs and device assignments.
- Requires input.flight-profiles@^1.0.0 for default controller layouts and profile normalization.
- Requires input.flight-actions@^1.0.0 for action display labels.
- Requires input.gamepad@^1.0.0 (optional) for live axis and button diagnostics and button capture; button names fall back to numbers when absent.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization resolves local pilot, flight profile, and flight action services, then provides the CONTROLLER_MENUS factory.
- create mounts into a caller-provided root element and injects scoped CSS styles. If no gamepads manager is supplied, it creates and owns an internal FlightGamepad when input.gamepad is present.
- open initializes tab views, synchronizes player selection, and starts a requestAnimationFrame loop to update live axis meters and button activity indicators.
- Closing the dialog or pressing Escape cancels active button captures and halts the animation loop.
- dispose detaches DOM markup, removes injected style elements, unbinds window listeners, and disposes any owned gamepad manager. Provider disposal closes all open menus with an AggregateError if cleanup fails.

## Remove or replace
Games without local multiplayer or customizable controller layouts can omit this module. To replace it, implement a platform-tier module providing input.controller-menu@1.0.0 with your custom controls UI.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: menu options, services, and markup conventions may change before API freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/controller-menu is a creator example implementing a minimal replacement provider for input.controller-menu@1.0.0 that manipulates local player preferences headlessly. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/controller-menu/module.json /tmp/controller-menu-example.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/controller-menu-example.spacemod

=== LAN discovery [spacelab.discovery-lan/usage]
Borrowed text transports and explicit greeting codecs; no world dependencies.


## What it does
This module scans local network addresses to discover active multiplayer game servers. It generates candidate endpoint lists across local subnet ranges, opens probe connections over a borrowed TransportProvider, reads the server's initial handshake greeting, and validates protocol identifier and universe model compatibility without importing world or physics logic.

## Provides and requires
- Provides net.lan-discovery@1.0.0 through the LAN_DISCOVERY key: a LanDiscoveryFactory instance.
- Requires no services at initialization. Probing requires a caller-supplied TransportProvider (such as net.websocket).

## Settings
- timeoutMs (default 800, from 1 to 60000, step 1): Maximum wait for one candidate server to return a compatible greeting in milliseconds. A scan can explicitly override this default.
- concurrency (default 32, from 1 to 64, step 1): Maximum number of candidate addresses probed together. Lower values reduce network load; higher values complete large scans sooner.
- maxGreetingLength (default 16384, from 1 to 65536, step 1): Maximum UTF-16 characters accepted in a discovery greeting. Larger session messages are handled by the transport, not discovery.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization instantiates and provides the LanDiscoveryFactory configured with active module settings.
- create(options) builds a dedicated LanDiscovery scanner bound to a transport, protocol ID, and greeting parser.
- candidates(hints, port) generates candidate address lists from subnet hints and common private network prefixes.
- probe(address, timeoutMs?, signal?) initiates a single handshake probe, closing the connection as soon as a greeting arrives or timeout expires.
- scan(candidates, options) coordinates worker pools up to the concurrency limit, invoking onfound and onprogress callbacks.
- Disposing the discovery instance or factory aborts all pending probe connections immediately.

## Remove or replace
Removing this module disables automated LAN server scanning in multiplayer menus. Manual IP entry, tab discovery, and WebRTC invite codes remain operational. To replace it, author a module providing net.lan-discovery@1.0.0 implementing LanDiscoveryFactory.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: discovery interfaces and candidate formats may be updated before release.

## Example
examples/modules/discovery-lan demonstrates generating LAN candidate addresses and scanning endpoints with a mock transport. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/discovery-lan/module.json /tmp/discovery-lan-example.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/discovery-lan-example.spacemod

=== Independent fleet views [spacelab.fleet-view/usage]
Selected-provider coast queries, per-view culling/cache and borrowed world reads.


## What it does
This module provides per-view fleet culling and render list generation for secondary vessels and unpiloted objects. It uses orbit-annulus geometric bounds to cull ships outside the camera view before computing expensive Kepler solves, and caches solved positions while sub-pixel motion remains below a threshold, maintaining high frame rates across large fleets.

## Provides and requires
- Provides view.fleet@1.0.0 through the FLEET_VIEWS key: creates FlightFleetView instances for per-view ship list construction.
- Fills the view.fleet slot in presentation profiles.
- Requires no external services in its module manifest.

## Settings
- marginPixels (default 40, from 40 to 256, step 1): Viewport margin in CSS pixels matching the renderer margin, avoiding visual pop-in at screen edges.
- stillPixels (default 0.25, from 0 to 1, step 0.05): Maximum CSS-pixel drift allowed before recomputing a cached coast position.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization captures marginPixels and stillPixels settings, contributes this guide, and registers FLEET_VIEWS.
- create() produces an independent FlightFleetView for each viewport.
- build(input) inspects borrowed world state, culls off-screen orbits, solves visible coast states, projects screen positions, and generates RenderShip records.
- dispose clears placed ship caches, label strings, and world references.

## Remove or replace
To replace fleet culling and rendering behaviors, provide view.fleet@1.0.0 in the view.fleet slot. Removing this module disables fleet presentation in multi-ship scenarios.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: fleet statistics structures and culling heuristics may evolve before API freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/fleet-view demonstrates instantiating a fleet view, submitting frame parameters, and reading culling statistics. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/fleet-view/module.json /tmp/example-fleet-view.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-fleet-view.spacemod

=== Shared flight plans [spacelab.flight-plans/usage]
A shared service that stores maneuver plans as portable intent and checks them against the selected physics.


## What it does
A flight plan is a list of maneuver nodes for one ship: at an absolute simulation time, add a velocity change made of prograde and radial parts, in m/s, around a named body. This module captures, validates and evaluates those plans. It stores intent only: no throttle, warp, arming or execution state. Evaluation uses the physics the game selected (motion, orbits, surfaces and rails) on a cloned context and never changes the live world.
Planners, transfer search and save files share one plan format, space-sim-flight-plans/1, so a plan made by one tool can be reviewed by another.

## Provides and requires
- Provides flight.plans@1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_PLANS key: a FlightPlanService.
- Requires no services. Callers pass a WorldRead and ship. Finite-burn checks also need a ManeuverBurnFactory, which the host binds with withBurns.
- Consumers in this checkout: spacelab.transfer-planner requires flight.plans@^1.0.0; spacelab.maneuver-editor declares it as optional; the Space Sim host uses it to validate plans stored in saves.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization provides one frozen, stateless service and this guide. The module owns no resources between calls.
- capture snapshots the ship's current coast, the world identity and its motion model. read validates an untrusted archive: at most 64 plans of 16 nodes, node times increasing and within 100 Julian years of the plan's start. Unknown fields are dropped, and invalid input throws an Error that starts with "Flight plan:".
- evaluate is a fast check for nodes around the starting body. A node on another body, a finite-burn node, or a coast that may cross an SOI or reach the surface first gets a reason asking for progressive evaluation.
- evaluateAsync and predict are generators that do a bounded amount of work per step. They follow coasts through SOI changes and surface contact. They need world.predictionFeatures.futureSeeds, and endTime when a coast has to be traced rather than solved directly; older hosts get a reason instead. Close a generator you stop early with return(): this releases its forecast and cloned motion context.
- Each evaluated node reports valid and a readable reason. A changed universe, system, physics fingerprint or motion model invalidates every node until the player reviews the plan; after the first failure, later nodes repeat its reason.

## Remove or replace
Space Sim enables this module but does not list flight.plans as a required slot. Without it, the transfer planner cannot be enabled because it requires the service, the maneuver editor keeps working without shared plans, and a save that contains flight plans fails to load with a message asking for the module.
A replacement provides flight.plans@1.0.0, lists flight.plans in slots and is selected with "providers": { "flight.plans": "your.module-id" }. It must still read space-sim-flight-plans/1 archives, so that existing saves and other planners stay compatible.

## Stability
Three versions apply here and change independently: the module version 0.1.0, the API identifier space-sim-game-api/2, and the archive format space-sim-flight-plans/1. The API and the manifest format, space-sim-module/2, are pre-freeze. A new archive format would need a migration so that older saves keep loading. Optional members of FlightPlanService, such as evaluateAsync and withBurns, may be missing on older providers; check for them before calling.

## Example
examples/modules/flight-plans declares flight.plans as an optional service. It summarizes an archive when the service is present, reports the validation message when an archive is rejected, and still works when no provider is enabled. Build and check it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/flight-plans/module.json /tmp/plan-summary.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/plan-summary.spacemod

=== Progressive flight prediction [spacelab.flight-prediction/usage]
Cancellable trajectory previews that a view can trace a little at a time without touching live flight.


## What it does
A prediction (also called a ghost path) is the future trajectory drawn ahead of a ship. This module creates one preview per view and traces it progressively: each rendered frame does a small, bounded amount of work, so long paths appear over several frames instead of stalling one. Tracing runs on an isolated copy of the motion model, or through the world's own read-only predictor, and never advances the live simulation.
All values are body-relative SI: positions in metres, velocities in m/s and times in seconds of simulation time. Each sample may name its active body (bodyId) and the frame it is expressed in (frameId), a translating, nonrotating frame centred on the starting body. Transform both position and velocity through the system's ephemerides before comparing samples from different frames.

## Provides and requires
- Provides view.prediction@1.0.0 through the PREDICTIONS key: a PredictionFactory whose create(onComplete) returns a FlightPrediction.
- Requires no services. Callers pass the SimulationContext, ship state, body and optional surface on every update, and optionally a WorldRead.
- Consumers in this checkout: the flight host (Player 1's preview), spacelab.flight-split (guest seats) and spacelab.maneuver-editor, which both require view.prediction@^1.0.0.

## Settings
- maxMs (default 3, from 0.1 to 16, step 0.1): wall-clock milliseconds of tracing work allowed in one update of one preview. A consumer may lower FlightPrediction.maxMs for a single preview, for example a split-screen guest. Results do not change, only how quickly they appear.
- traceSamplesPerSecond (default 1000, from 1 to 1000, step 1): samples of work earned per second of elapsed wall time for analytic coast previews.
- numericalSamplesPerSecond (default 12000, from 120 to 24000, step 1): samples of work earned per second for previews that need step-by-step integration, such as terrain contact or shared-world rules.
- Each update allows between 128 and 256 samples of work and stops early when maxMs is used up.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization provides the factory and registers it with context.own, so disposing the candidate disposes every preview it created.
- create(onComplete) returns an independent preview with its own job, budget and two reusable sample buffers. onComplete runs once for each finished pass.
- update starts a new pass when the ship, its status, the body, the surface, the motion model or a relevant command changes; otherwise it continues the current pass. While a replacement is tracing, path shows only the new partial path, never the stale one.
- Pass world to trace through sphere-of-influence (SOI) changes with the host's world.predictShip; omit it for a body-only preview of the given state. In body-only mode, escape means the preview's distance limit was reached.
- Read path and pathEnd. pathEnd is closed (a full orbit), impact, floating, escape, horizon (the predictor's time limit), open (a work limit, never a physical end), failed or grounded. A pass that reaches 32,768 samples ends as open.
- invalidate cancels the pass and clears the path. Cancelling, finishing, a predictor error and dispose all close the job and its cloned context, even if one cleanup step fails. A disposed preview throws on update.
The path array is reused by a later pass. Copy samples you need to keep beyond the next update.

## Remove or replace
Space Sim and Flight starter list view.prediction as a required slot, and split screen and the maneuver editor require it, so those games need a provider. A game that requires none of them can leave prediction out: flight, saves and multiplayer do not depend on it.
A replacement provides view.prediction@1.0.0 with its own PredictionFactory, lists view.prediction in slots, and is selected with "providers": { "view.prediction": "your.module-id" }. It must keep the same rules: no writes to the world, a bounded amount of work per update, and closing every job on invalidate and dispose.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze. FlightPrediction still exposes diagnostic fields such as passKey and lastPass that existing consumers read; treat them as diagnostics that may change before the API freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/flight-prediction consumes view.prediction: it creates a preview, updates it until the pass finishes or a frame limit is reached, copies the result and disposes the preview. Build and check it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/flight-prediction/module.json /tmp/prediction-probe.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/prediction-probe.spacemod

## Related
-> Code modules: manifests, packaging and installation [modules]
-> game-api flight-view.ts: PredictionFactory and FlightPrediction [api-reference/game-api/flight-view]
-> Determinism: why previews never change the live world [determinism]
-> API reference: Progressive flight prediction [api-reference/module/spacelab.flight-prediction]

=== Flight split screen [spacelab.flight-split/usage]
Independent local flight seats and split/dynamic panes over a borrowed session.


## What it does
This module provides dynamic and fixed splitscreen flight views for up to four local players. When players fly together, they share a single adaptive camera pane; as vessels diverge past distance or screen thresholds, the viewport splits smoothly into independent player panes with dedicated cameras, trajectory predictions, fleet culling, and gamepad assignments.

## Provides and requires
- Provides view.flight-split@1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_SPLIT service key: creates FlightSplitScreen controllers managing guest seating and viewports.
- Requires input.local-pilots@^1.0.0 through the LOCAL_PILOTS key: queries player color and profile preferences.
- Requires input.flight-profiles@^1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_PROFILES key: manages controller mapping profiles.
- Requires view.flight-camera@^1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_CAMERAS key: creates independent flight cameras for guest viewports.
- Requires view.prediction@^1.0.0 through the PREDICTIONS key: allocates independent progressive trajectory previews.
- Requires view.fleet@^1.0.0 through the FLEET_VIEWS key: builds per-pane fleet ship visibility lists.
- Requires view.local@^1.0.0 through the LOCAL_VIEWS key: calculates responsive split pane rectangles.
- Requires input.gamepad@^1.0.0 (optional) through the GAMEPAD_INPUT key: enables controller polling for guest joining.
- Fills the view.flight-split slot in platform configurations.

## Settings
- joinButton (default 9, from 0 to 31, step 1): Standard gamepad button index that lets an unclaimed controller join local play. Default 9 is Start/Menu/Options; ownership rules still apply.
- guestPredictionMs (default 1.5, from 0.1 to 10, step 0.1): Wall-time prediction budget for each guest view. Higher budgets refine previews sooner; they do not advance or alter the shared world.
- wheelZoom (default 0.0015, from 0.0001 to 0.01, step 0.0001): Exponential zoom factor per mouse-wheel unit in guest panes. Each guest camera remains independent.
- separateScreenRatio (default 0.55, from 0.1 to 1, step 0.01): Split a guest into its own pane when its screen distance exceeds this fraction of the viewport’s smaller dimension.
- mergeScreenRatio (default 0.35, from 0.05 to 1, step 0.01): A separated guest must fall below this screen-distance fraction before merging. Must not exceed the separation threshold.
- separateDistance (default 5000, from 1 to 1000000, step 1): Split a guest when its body-relative distance from Player 1 exceeds this distance, even if the shared camera is zoomed far out.
- mergeDistance (default 3500, from 1 to 1000000, step 1): A guest must be closer than this distance before merging. Must not exceed the world separation threshold.
- mergeDelayMs (default 600, from 0 to 10000, step 1): Keep a guest inside both merge thresholds for this long before returning to the shared view. Prevents rapid split/merge flicker.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization validates separation and merge threshold constraints, binds required input and camera services, contributes this guide, and registers FLIGHT_SPLIT.
- create(viewport, host) mounts the splitscreen container and wires session callbacks.
- pollPads() checks for join button presses on unassigned controllers and seats new players.
- updateDynamic evaluates inter-ship separation and switches between single-view centroid tracking and multi-pane layouts.
- dispose cleans up active guest cameras, prediction engines, fleet views, and gamepad instances.

## Remove or replace
To customize multiplayer flight presentation, implement view.flight-split@1.0.0 in the view.flight-split slot. Removing this module disables local multi-seat flight, restricting games to single-player viewports.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: split-screen host interfaces and dynamic centroid algorithms may be refined prior to API freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/flight-split demonstrates instantiating the flight split-screen manager and configuring local player seats. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/flight-split/module.json /tmp/example-flight-split.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-flight-split.spacemod

=== HUD layout and visibility [spacelab.hud-layout/usage]
Optional viewer layout, movement and visibility with complete cleanup.


## What it does
This module provides responsive HUD placement and interactive layout editing for flight instruments and UI controls. It enables dragging and keyboard repositioning of HUD elements, fits flexible instruments around obstacles, and provides a visibility controller for toggling individual instruments with persistent theme preferences.

## Provides and requires
- Provides ui.hud-layout@1.0.0 through the HUD_LAYOUT key: creates HudLayout responsive positioning controllers and HudVisibilityController managers.
- Requires ui.windows@^1.0.0 through the WINDOWS key: uses window system icon assets for layout handles.
- Fills the ui.hud-layout slot in platform profiles.

## Settings
- moveStepPx (default 8, from 1 to 128, step 1): Distance moved by one arrow-key press on a focused HUD grip in CSS pixels. Saved positions remain normalized to the viewport.
- fastMoveStepPx (default 32, from 1 to 128, step 1): Distance moved by Shift plus an arrow key on a focused HUD grip in CSS pixels. Does not change pointer dragging or world coordinates.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization binds WINDOWS, loads layout stylesheets, captures moveStepPx and fastMoveStepPx, contributes this guide, and registers HUD_LAYOUT.
- create(options) mounts layout styling and manages obstacle avoidance and drag bounds across viewport canvases.
- visibility(options) instantiates visibility controllers managing toggle checkboxes and persisting display choices.
- dispose cleanly detaches injected style elements, listeners, and interactive layout handles.

## Remove or replace
To customize HUD layout behavior, provide an alternative implementation of ui.hud-layout@1.0.0 in the ui.hud-layout slot. Removing this module disables interactive HUD rearranging and visibility panels, leaving instruments at their default fixed coordinates.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: layout coordinate normalization formulas and visibility storage schemas may be adjusted prior to API freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/hud-layout demonstrates configuring a HUD layout controller and obstacle avoidance. Build and validate it from the repository root:
